A two coordinate model in which the sufficiency gap hypotheses depend on the summary

Proposition 15.45, page 264 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: counterexample, proved.

Statement. Let \(\theta\in\mathbb{R}^2\) have the prior \(N(0,s_N^2\mathrm{Id})\) with \(s_N\to0\) and \(Ns_N^2\to\infty\), and let \(Y_i=G(\theta)+\varepsilon_i\), \(i\le N\), with independent \(\varepsilon_i\sim N(0,\mathrm{Id})\) and \(G(\theta)=(\theta_1+\theta_2^2,\theta_2)\). The mean \(X\) of the \(Y_i\) is sufficient, \(DG(\theta)\) has rows \((1,2\theta_2)\) and \((0,1)\), and \((DG(\theta)^\top DG(\theta))^{-1}\) has first diagonal entry \(1+4\theta_2^2\). Let \(\mathscr{W}_1\) be the Wasserstein distance of order one on \(\mathbb{R}^2\) with the Euclidean norm and \(\mathrm{dist}(V,V')=\max_{|u|=1}|(u^\top Vu)^{1/2}-(u^\top V'u)^{1/2}|\). A linear Gaussian model on \(\mathbb{R}^2\) is \(Y_G=\Phi_G\theta_G+\varepsilon_G\) with a prior \(N(0,\Sigma_0)\) and \(\varepsilon_G\sim N(0,\mathrm{Id})\), with posterior \(\Pi_G\) and posterior covariance \(V_Z\) given \(Y_G\); for \(\theta_\ast\in\mathbb{R}^2\) the Gaussian model at \(\theta_\ast\) is \(Y_G=\sqrt N\,DG(\theta_\ast)\theta_G+\varepsilon_G\) with the prior \(N(0,s_N^2\mathrm{Id})\). For a statistic \(S\) of \(X\), a linear statistic \(S_G\) of \(Y_G\) and a coupling, the first hypothesis of the nonlinear sufficiency gap bounds is \(\sqrt N\,E\,\mathscr{W}_1(\Pi(\cdot\mid X),\Pi_G(\cdot\mid Y_G))\to0\) and the second is \(\sqrt N\,E\,\mathscr{W}_1(\Pi(\cdot\mid S),\Pi_G(\cdot\mid S_G))\to0\); those bounds hold in this setting with the same proof. Then:

  1. coupled through \(\theta_G=\theta\) and \(\varepsilon_G=\sqrt N(X-G(\theta))\), the Gaussian model at the prior mean \(\theta_\ast=0\) satisfies the first hypothesis;

  2. for every linear Gaussian model on \(\mathbb{R}^2\) and every coupling, \(\liminf_N\sqrt N\,E\,\mathscr{W}_1(\Pi(\cdot\mid X),\Pi_G(\cdot\mid Y_G))\ge\sqrt{2/\pi}\,\liminf_N\mathrm{dist}(NV_Z,\mathrm{Id})\); for a point \(\theta_0\in\mathbb{R}^2\) the Gaussian model at \(\theta_\ast=\theta_0\) has \(NV_Z\to(DG(\theta_0)^\top DG(\theta_0))^{-1}\), so it fails the first hypothesis whenever \(\theta_{0,2}\ne0\), by at least \(\sqrt{2/\pi}\,((1+4\theta_{0,2}^2)^{1/2}-1)\);

  3. let \(S=X_1\), the first coordinate of the adjoint statistic \(DG(0)^\top X\) at the prior mean, which is also the one step summary \(\bar\theta+DG(\bar\theta)^{-1}(X-G(\bar\theta))\) from \(\bar\theta=0\); if \(\sqrt Ns_N^2\to\infty\), then for every linear Gaussian model on \(\mathbb{R}^2\), every linear statistic \(S_G\) and every coupling, \(\sqrt N\,E\,\mathscr{W}_1(\Pi(\cdot\mid S),\Pi_G(\cdot\mid S_G))\to\infty\), and its ratio to \(\sqrt Ns_N^2\) has \(\liminf\) at least \(c_\ast=\sup_{0<\tau\le1/2}\kappa_\tau/(2(1-\tau))\), where \(\kappa_\tau=\big|\sqrt{2x_\tau/\pi}\,e^{-x_\tau/2}-\sqrt{2x_{1-\tau}/\pi}\,e^{-x_{1-\tau}/2}\big|\) and \(x_\tau\) is the \(\tau\) quantile of the \(\chi^2_1\) law (numerically \(c_\ast=0.1588\), attained near \(\tau=0.204\));

  4. let \(S'=X_1-X_2^2\), the first coordinate of the maximum likelihood estimator \(G^{-1}(X)\), which is also the one step summary from any preliminary estimate with \(\bar\theta_2=X_2\), and let \(S_G=Y_{G,1}/\sqrt N\) in the Gaussian model at \(\theta_\ast=0\), coupled as in (a); then \(\sqrt N\,E\,\mathscr{W}_1(\Pi(\cdot\mid S'),\Pi_G(\cdot\mid S_G))\to0\), so both hypotheses hold for \(S'\) with this model and coupling.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m2c.tex, label m:prop:gaptoy.

Effective dimension under dissipation

Theorem 15.46, page 267 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: conditional on Part (ii) for a nonlinear forward map assumes the hypotheses of the book’s Theorem 16.10 (Bernstein von Mises for the identity skip quantile generator), among them the uniform Bernstein von Mises assumption (Assumption U, open: Open Question 17.18), the whitened training residual assumption (U2’) (the whitened training residuals \(\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\sqrt N(\theta^{(m)}-\hat S^{(m)}_N)\), \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\), split, conditionally i.i.d. given the data, into a part whose projections on unit vectors have sub Gaussian norms given the data bounded in probability uniformly over the unit vectors and whose conditional second moment matrix tends in operator norm to the whitened limit covariance \(\mathrm{Id}+\Xi\), and a remainder whose conditional mean square norm tends to zero, all in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability; the book proves it for training parameters drawn from a proposal localized at the preliminary estimate, under the other hypotheses, and it fails in general for a nonlinear forward map when they are drawn from the prior) and the error bounds \(\|\hat S_N-\theta_0\|_{L^2}=o_P(\tilde\delta_N(0))\), with \(\tilde\delta_N(0)\) the posterior contraction rate in \(L^2\), and \(\|\hat S_N-\theta_0\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}=o_P(1)\), \(\bar\gamma\ge2\), on the summary, which the book leaves open for a nonlinear forward map other than reaction diffusion when the preliminary estimates have only the rates of the summary’s definition; parts (i) and (iii) and the linear case of part (ii) assume nothing unproved..

Statement. Consider the heat equation, or the Stokes equation on divergence free fields, on the torus \(\mathbb{T}^d=[0,1]^d\) with viscosity \(\nu\), Laplace eigenpairs \((e_j,\lambda_j)\) with \(\lambda_j=4\pi^2|k|^2\) for the \(j\)th lattice mode \(k\in\mathbb{Z}^d\setminus\{0\}\), \(N\) noisy observations of the solution at points uniform on \([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^d\), per mode information \(\iota_j=(1-e^{-2\nu\lambda_jT})/(2\nu\lambda_jT)\), and prior variances \(\sigma^2_{0,j}=\rho^2(\lambda_j/\lambda_1)^{-\alpha}\) for the coefficients \(\langle e_j,\theta\rangle\) of the initial condition. Define the effective dimension \(J_{\rm eff}=\#\{j:N\iota_j\sigma^2_{0,j}\ge1\}\), the number of modes in which the data at least halve the prior variance. Then:

  1. \(J_{\rm eff}=\#\{j:\lambda_j^{\alpha+1}\le c_jN\rho^2\lambda_1^\alpha/(2\nu T)\}\) with \(c_j=1-e^{-2\nu\lambda_jT}\in[1-e^{-2\nu\lambda_1T},1]\); hence, as \(\Lambda=(N\rho^2\lambda_1^\alpha/(2\nu T))^{1/(\alpha+1)}\to\infty\) with \(\nu T\Lambda\ge1\), and with constants that depend only on \(\alpha\), \(d\) and \(\rho\), \(J_{\rm eff}\asymp(N\rho^2/(\nu T))^{d/(2(\alpha+1))}\asymp N^{d/(2\alpha+2)}(\nu T)^{-d/(2\alpha+2)}\). For \(d=2\) this is \(N^{1/(\alpha+1)}\), which for \(\alpha=\gamma\) is the order of the dimension \(J_N=N\delta_N^2=N^{1/(\gamma+1)}\), \(\delta_N=N^{-\gamma/(2\gamma+d)}\), of the summary in Nickl’s (2024) Bernstein von Mises theorem; at fixed \(N\), viscosity or observation time reduces the effective dimension polynomially once \(\nu T\ge\lambda_1^{-1}(2/(N\rho^2))^{1/\alpha}\), and for every \(\nu T\) the count is at most \(\#\{j:\lambda_j\le\lambda_1(N\rho^2)^{1/\alpha}\}\), because \(\iota_j\le1\).

  2. In the linear model (the model truncated to \(D\) modes, \(Y=\Phi\theta+\varepsilon\) with unit noise variance, prior covariance \(\Sigma_0\) and posterior covariance \(V_N=(\Phi^\top\Phi+\Sigma_0^{-1})^{-1}\)), the identity skip generator, which adds to the summary a fixed linear map of standard Gaussian noise, is exact to leading order with the analytic scale \((N\iota_j+\sigma_{0,j}^{-2})^{-1/2}\), is exact with the scale \(V_N^{1/2}\), and requires no training. For a nonlinear forward map whose information operator has the form \(\mathbb{I}^*\mathbb{I}=\mathcal{L}^*\mathcal{L}+\mathcal{K}\), with \(\mathcal{L}\) the Stokes (heat) solution operator and \(\mathcal{K}\) compact and gaining two derivatives, assume the hypotheses of the book’s Bernstein von Mises theorem for the identity skip quantile generator, namely Conditions 1 and 2 of Nickl (2024), the uniform Bernstein von Mises assumption for the one step summary \(\hat S_N\), the whitened training residual assumption (U2’), the hypotheses under which \(\hat S_N\) is \(\sqrt N\) equivalent to the efficient centering, and error bounds on \(\hat S_N\). Write the limit covariance on the first \(J_N\) modes as \(\Sigma=\mathcal{D}^{1/2}(\mathrm{Id}+\Xi)\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\) with \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\), where \(\Xi\) is the compression to the first \(J_N\) modes of the compact operator \(\Xi_\infty=(\mathrm{Id}+K')^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}\), \(K'=T^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{K}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\), so that \(\|\Xi P_{>J}\|_{op}\le\|\Xi_\infty P_{>J}\|_{op}\to0\) as \(J\to\infty\) uniformly in \(N\), with \(P_{>J}\) the projection onto the modes \(j>J\). The generator of that theorem is \(\widehat G(s,U)=s+N^{-1/2}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\hat C_M^{1/2}q(U)\), with \(U\) uniform on \([0,1]^{J_N}\), \(q\) the standard normal quantile function applied coordinatewise, and \(\hat C_M\) the uncentered second moment matrix of the whitened training residuals \(\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\sqrt N(\theta^{(m)}-\hat S^{(m)}_N)\), \(m\le M\). Given a cut \(J'=J'_N\le J_N\) with \(J'\to\infty\), the hybrid generator replaces the head \(\hat C_M^{1/2}\) by \((\hat C^{\rm hyb}_M)^{1/2}\), where \(\hat C^{\rm hyb}_M=\operatorname{diag}(\hat C^{J'}_M,\mathrm{Id})\) and \(\hat C^{J'}_M\) is the second moment matrix of the first \(J'\) whitened coordinates, so that it learns the correction to the analytic scale \(\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\) on the first \(J'\) modes only and uses the analytic scale beyond. Then \(\|\hat C^{\rm hyb}_M-(\mathrm{Id}+\Xi)\|_{op}\le O_P\big(\sqrt{J'/M}+J'/M\big)+2\|\Xi_\infty P_{>J'}\|_{op}+o_P(1)\), and the conclusion of that theorem (the GBC posterior \(\hat\Pi\) satisfies \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}(\hat\Pi,\Pi_N)\to0\) in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability, \(\Pi_N\) being the posterior and \(\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\) the Wasserstein distance of order one on the negative Sobolev space \(H^{-k-2}\), together with the tail and moment condition (G2) of the book’s transfer theorem) holds for the hybrid generator with \(J'/M_N\to0\) in place of \(J_N/M_N\to0\); with the cut \(J'=\min(J_{\rm eff},J_N)\) it suffices that \(J_{\rm eff}/M_N\to0\).

  3. In the Gaussian experiment that observes \(\theta_j+(N\iota_j)^{-1/2}\xi_j\) with independent standard normal \(\xi_j\) and independent priors \(\theta_j\sim N(0,\sigma^2_{0,j})\), a generator that reproduces the posterior center exactly but uses the prior spread for the modes \(j>J'\) incurs a Wasserstein penalty, of order one in the Euclidean norm, of at most \(\sqrt{2/\pi}\sum_{j>J'}\sigma_{0,j}\big(1-(1+N\iota_j\sigma^2_{0,j})^{-1/2}\big)\le\sqrt{1/(2\pi)}\sum_{j>J'}N\iota_j\sigma^3_{0,j}\), which decays like \((J')^{1-(3\alpha+2)/d}\) when the sum is replaced by its integral.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m2c.tex, label m:thm:jeff.

Sufficiency versus the raw data: simulation cost of a generator trained without a summary

Theorem 15.47, page 269 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: conditional on The second claim (the hybrid generator trained on the sufficient statistic) assumes the hypotheses of part (ii) of the effective dimension theorem, which include those of the book’s Theorem 16.10: the uniform Bernstein von Mises assumption (Assumption U, open: Open Question 17.18), the whitened training residual assumption (U2’) (the whitened training residuals \(\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\sqrt N(\theta^{(m)}-\hat S^{(m)}_N)\), \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\), split, conditionally i.i.d. given the data, into a part whose projections on unit vectors have sub Gaussian norms given the data bounded in probability uniformly over the unit vectors and whose conditional second moment matrix tends in operator norm to the whitened limit covariance \(\mathrm{Id}+\Xi\), and a remainder whose conditional mean square norm tends to zero, all in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability; the book proves it for training parameters drawn from a proposal localized at the preliminary estimate, under the other hypotheses, and it fails in general for a nonlinear forward map when they are drawn from the prior) and the error bounds \(\|\hat S_N-\theta_0\|_{L^2}=o_P(\tilde\delta_N(0))\), with \(\tilde\delta_N(0)\) the posterior contraction rate in \(L^2\), and \(\|\hat S_N-\theta_0\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}=o_P(1)\), \(\bar\gamma\ge2\), on the summary, which the book leaves open for a nonlinear forward map other than reaction diffusion when the preliminary estimates have only the rates of the summary’s definition. The first claim, the lower bound on the number of simulations, is unconditional..

Statement. Let a generator be trained on \(M\) i.i.d. simulated pairs to reproduce the conditional law of \(\theta\) given the raw observation vector \(Y\in\mathbb{R}^N\) (or \(\mathbb{R}^{2N}\) for a vector field), with no summary, and let it be required to be accurate uniformly over the class \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(N)\) of conditional quantile maps \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) on \([0,1]\times[0,1]^N\) that have Hölder norm of order \(\beta\) at most one and are nondecreasing in \(\tau\), the inputs having a law on \([0,1]^N\) with a Lebesgue density bounded above by \(C_\mu\) and below by a positive constant. Then the number of simulations needed for \(\sqrt N\,E\,\mathscr{W}_1(\hat\Pi,\Pi(\cdot\mid Y))\to0\), with \(\hat\Pi\) the law the generator reports and \(\mathscr{W}_1\) the Wasserstein distance of order one, satisfies \(M\ge cN^{(2\beta+N)/(2\beta)}\) for all large \(N\), where \(c=C_\mu^{-1}\); the right side grows faster than any exponential in \(N\). For the heat or Stokes model with prior variances \(\sigma^2_{0,j}=\rho^2(\lambda_j/\lambda_1)^{-\alpha}\), viscosity \(\nu\), observation window \([0,T]\) and effective dimension \(J_{\rm eff}=\#\{j:N\iota_j\sigma^2_{0,j}\ge1\}\asymp N^{d/(2\alpha+2)}(\nu T)^{-d/(2\alpha+2)}\) (catalog entry effective_dimension_under_dissipation), the hybrid generator of part (ii) of that entry with the cut \(\min(J_{\rm eff},J_N)\), trained on the sufficient statistic, which uses the Bernstein von Mises structure that the uniform class does not, satisfies the accuracy condition (G1), \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}(\hat\Pi,\Pi_N)\to0\) in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability with \(\Pi_N\) the posterior, as soon as \(J_{\rm eff}/M\to0\), conditionally on the hypotheses of part (ii) of that entry, which include those of the book’s Theorem 16.10 and the uniform Bernstein von Mises assumption.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m2c.tex, label m:thm:raw.

Chapter 16. Transfer, Generators and Localized Training

Transfer of Bernstein von Mises guarantees to the GBC posterior (BvM for GBC)

Theorem 16.2, page 275 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Setting. Let \(\Omega=[0,1]^d\) be the torus, \(T>0\) and \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\). The data are \(Z^{(N)}=(Y_i,X_i)_{i\le N}\) with \(Y_i=u_\theta(X_i)+\varepsilon_i\), where \(u_\theta=\mathscr{G}(\theta)\) solves an evolution equation with initial condition \(\theta\), the \(X_i\) are i.i.d. uniform on \(\mathcal{X}\) and the \(\varepsilon_i\sim N(0,I_W)\) are independent of them. Let \((e_j)\) be the Laplace eigenbasis, with eigenvalues \(\lambda_j\asymp j^{2/d}\); let the prior \(\Pi\) be the law of \((\sqrt N\delta_N)^{-1}\sum_j\lambda_j^{-\gamma/2}g_je_j\) with \(g_j\) i.i.d. \(N(0,1)\) and \(\delta_N=N^{-\gamma/(2\gamma+d)}\); let \(\Pi_N=\Pi(\cdot\mid Z^{(N)})\) be the posterior, \(\theta_0\) the true initial condition and \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) the law of the data. Let \(\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0}\) be the linearization of \(\mathscr{G}\) at \(\theta_0\) and \(\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}=N(0,T(\mathbb{I}^*_{\theta_0}\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0})^{-1})\). Assume Conditions 1 and 2 of Nickl (2024): the prior above and, for the forward map on Sobolev balls, (A) uniform \(L^\infty\) bounds, (B) \(L^2\) Lipschitz continuity, (C) a linearization \(\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0}\) with quadratic remainder, (D) \(L^\infty\) mapping properties from \(H^\zeta\) with \(\zeta>d/2\), (E) the stability estimate \(\|\mathscr{G}(\theta)-\mathscr{G}(\theta_0)\|_{L^2(\mathcal{X})}\ge c\|\theta-\theta_0\|_{H^{-1}}\) and (F) invertibility of the information operator (\(\Delta\mathbb{I}^*_{\theta_0}\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0}\) a homeomorphism of \(H^\eta_0\) for \(\eta\ge\eta_0\)), with integer \(\gamma>\max(3\zeta+3d/2+2,\eta_0)\) and \(k>\max(\zeta+5d/2,\eta_0)\).

Accuracy conditions. Let \(\mathscr{W}_1\) be the Wasserstein distance of order one on \(H^{-k-2}(\Omega)\). Let \(\bar\gamma\) be the Sobolev index of the localization ball, chosen as in the proofs of Nickl (2024) and of Konen and Nickl (2026) in \(\max(2,2\zeta)<\bar\gamma<\gamma-\max(1,d/2)\); put \(\tilde\delta_N(\xi)=\delta_N^{(\bar\gamma-\xi)/(\bar\gamma+1)}\), \(\bar\Theta_{N,L}=\{\|\theta\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}\le L\}\cap\{\|\theta-\theta_0\|_{H^\xi}\le L\tilde\delta_N(\xi)\ \text{for all}\ 0\le\xi<\bar\gamma\}\), and \(q=4\) in the setting of Theorem 1 of Nickl (2024), \(q=4\bar\gamma\) in that of Theorem 1.1 of Konen and Nickl (2026). Let \(\widehat\Pi_N\) be the GBC posterior (the law of the generator’s output at the observed data, a measurable function of \(Z^{(N)}\) and of simulation randomness drawn independently of \(Z^{(N)}\)) and \(\hat\theta^{G}_N\) its mean. Assume that, in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability as \(N\to\infty\): (G1) \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_1(\widehat\Pi_N,\Pi_N)\to0\); (G2) for some \(b>0\) and \(L\), \(\widehat\Pi_N(\bar\Theta_{N,L}^c)\le e^{-bN\delta_N^2}\) and \(\mathbb{E}^{\widehat\Pi_N}\|\theta\|^q_{H^{\bar\gamma}}=O_P(1)\).

Then: (i) \(\mathscr{W}_1\big(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt N(\theta-\hat\theta^{G}_N)\mid\theta\sim\widehat\Pi_N),\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\big)\to0\) in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability, and \(\sqrt N(\hat\theta^{G}_N-\theta_0)\to\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\) in distribution in \(H^{-k-2}\). (ii) If in addition the forward map satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem 1 of Nickl (2024) (the reaction diffusion equation \(\partial_tu-\Delta u=f(u)\) with \(f\in C^\infty_c(\mathbb{R})\), \(d\le3\), integer \(\gamma>2+3d\) and \(\theta_0\) smooth, with \(\mathscr{C}=C([t_{\min},t_{\max}],C(\Omega))\) for \(0<t_{\min}<t_{\max}<\infty\)) or of Theorem 1.1 of Konen and Nickl (2026) (the two dimensional Navier Stokes equations, with the space of trajectories \(\mathscr{C}\) of that theorem), and \(\mu\) is the law in \(\mathscr{C}\) of the solution \(U\) of the equation linearized at \(u_{\theta_0}\) with \(U(0)\sim\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\), then \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathscr{C}}\big(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt N(u_\theta-u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})\mid\theta\sim\widehat\Pi_N),\mu\big)\to0\) in probability and \(\sqrt N(u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N}-u_{\theta_0})\to\mu\) in distribution in \(\mathscr{C}\). (iii) Under (ii), for \(\Phi:\mathscr{C}\to\mathbb{B}\) Hadamard differentiable into a Banach space \(\mathbb{B}\), \(\sqrt N(\Phi(u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})-\Phi(u_{\theta_0}))\to\dot\Phi_{u_{\theta_0}}[U]\) in distribution, \(U\sim\mu\), and the GBC credible band \(C^{G}_N=\{u\in\mathscr{C}:\|u-u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\le R_N\}\), with \(R_N\) the \(1-\beta\) quantile of \(\|u_\theta-u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\) under \(\theta\sim\widehat\Pi_N\), satisfies \(P^N_{\theta_0}(u_{\theta_0}\in C^{G}_N)\to1-\beta\), and \(\sqrt N R_N\to c>0\). (iv) In the Navier Stokes case of (ii), let \(\theta_{N,h}=\theta_0+h/\sqrt N\), and let \(\dot H^{\infty}_\diamond\) be the smooth, divergence free, mean zero vector fields on the torus. For every \(h\in\dot H^{\infty}_\diamond\), under \(P^N_{\theta_{N,h}}\), \(\sqrt N(\hat\theta^{G}_N-\theta_{N,h})\to\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\) in distribution in \(H^{-k-2}\) and \(\sqrt N(u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N}-u_{\theta_{N,h}})\to\mu\) in distribution in \(\mathscr{C}\); that is, \(u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N}\) is a regular estimator sequence of the state at \(\theta_0\) whose limit law \(\mu\) attains the bound of Theorem 1.2 of Konen and Nickl (2026) (Theorem 2.10 there for a general \(\Phi\)). Consequently, with \(\Phi\) as in (iii) and \(\Phi=\operatorname{Id}\) permitted, \(\Phi(u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})\) is efficient among regular estimator sequences by the convolution theorem, and for every bounded, continuous, subconvex loss \(\ell:\mathbb{B}\to[0,\infty)\) it attains the local asymptotic minimax bound: \[\sup_{J\subset\dot H^{\infty}_\diamond,\ |J|<\infty}\ \liminf_{N\to\infty}\ \max_{h\in J}\ \mathbb{E}^N_{\theta_{N,h}}\,\ell\Big(\sqrt N\big(\Phi(u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})-\Phi(u_{\theta_{N,h}})\big)\Big)=\mathbb{E}\,\ell\big(\dot\Phi_{u_{\theta_0}}[U]\big),\qquad U\sim\mu.\]

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3a.tex, label m:thm:main.

Solve then simulate equals simulate the limit: tangent linear forward simulation of GBC draws

Corollary 16.4, page 280 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: conditional on Only the plug in bound of the second part is conditional: its proof uses the set \(\Theta_N\) and the Lipschitz clause (U3) of the uniform Bernstein von Mises assumption (Assumption U, stated clause by clause in the entry jiang_wong_under_bernstein_von_mises), which the book does not prove; the first part, and the rate for the GBC mean in the Sobolev norm of Condition 2D, whose proof uses only the transfer theorem and the moment clause of (G2), need only the hypotheses of the transfer theorem..

Statement. Let \(\widehat\Pi_N\) be a GBC posterior with mean \(\hat\theta^{G}_N\) that satisfies the hypotheses of part (ii) of the transfer theorem (catalog entry bvm_for_gbc): data \(Y_i=u_\theta(X_i)+\varepsilon_i\) on \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\) with Nickl’s rescaled Gaussian series prior and posterior \(\Pi_N\), Conditions 1 and 2 of Nickl (2024) with integer \(\gamma>\max(3\zeta+3d/2+2,\eta_0)\) and \(k>\max(\zeta+5d/2,\eta_0)\), the accuracy conditions (G1) \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}(\widehat\Pi_N,\Pi_N)\to0\) and (G2) (mass at most \(e^{-bN\delta_N^2}\) off the localization set and \(\mathbb{E}^{\widehat\Pi_N}\|\theta\|^q_{H^{\bar\gamma}}=O_P(1)\), with \(\bar\gamma\), \(\delta_N\) and \(q\) as in that entry), and the hypotheses of Theorem 1 of Nickl (2024) (reaction diffusion) or of Theorem 1.1 of Konen and Nickl (2026) (two dimensional Navier Stokes), with \(\mathscr{C}\) the space of trajectories of those theorems. Let \(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\) be the linearization of \(\theta\mapsto u_\theta\) at \(\vartheta\), \(\mathcal{N}_\vartheta=N(0,T(\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta)^{-1})\), and let \(\mu\) be the law of the solution of the tangent linear equation linearized at \(u_{\theta_0}\) with initial datum drawn from \(\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\). Then the law of \(\sqrt N(u_\theta-u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})\) obtained by pushing \(\theta\sim\widehat\Pi_N\) through the nonlinear solver and the law \(\mu\) obtained by pushing \(\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\) through the tangent linear equation are asymptotically equal in \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathscr{C}}\).

Suppose in addition that Assumption U (uniform Bernstein von Mises) holds with a set \(\Theta_N\) that contains \(\hat\theta^{G}_N\) with probability tending to one: for the summary \(S_N\), (U1) \(\Pi(\Theta_N)\to1\), \(\theta_0\in\Theta_N\) for all large \(N\) and \(\sup_{\vartheta\in\Theta_N}\mathbb{E}^N_\vartheta\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\big(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt N(\theta-S_N)\mid Z^{(N)}),\mathcal{N}_\vartheta\big)\to0\); (U2) these discrepancies are uniformly integrable under the prior predictive law; (U3) \(\vartheta\mapsto T(\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta)^{-1}\) is Lipschitz from \((\Theta_N,\|\cdot\|_{H^\zeta})\) into bounded operators \(H^{-k-2}\to H^{-k-4}\) and \(\vartheta\mapsto\mathcal{N}_\vartheta\) is Lipschitz from \((\Theta_N,\|\cdot\|_{H^\zeta})\) into the laws on \(H^{-k-2}\) with the distance \(\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\), with constants free of \(N\), the first moments \(m_1(\vartheta)=\int\|x\|_{H^{-k-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{N}_\vartheta(x)\) satisfy \(\int_{\Theta_N^c}m_1\,\mathrm{d}\Pi\to0\) and \(\Pi(\Theta_N^c)\sup_{\Theta_N}m_1\to0\), \(S_N\in\Theta_N\) with probability tending to one and \(\mathbb{E}^\Pi[\|\theta-S_N\|_{H^\zeta}\mid S_N]\to0\); (U4) for every \(\theta_0\in\Theta_N\), \(\mathbb{E}\big[\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\big(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt N(\theta-S_N)\mid Z^{(N)}),\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\big)\mid S_N\big]\to0\) in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability. Suppose also that, for the index \(\zeta>d/2\) of Condition 2D of Nickl (2024), \(\vartheta\mapsto\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\) is Lipschitz from an \(H^\zeta\) ball about \(\theta_0\) into the bounded operators \(H^{-k-2}\to\mathscr{C}\). Then \[\varrho_N:=\|\hat\theta^{G}_N-\theta_0\|_{H^\zeta}=O_P\big(N^{-(\bar\gamma-\zeta)/(2(\bar\gamma+k+2))}\big)\to0,\] and, writing \(U^\vartheta\) for the solution of the tangent equation linearized at \(u_\vartheta\) with initial datum drawn from \(\mathcal{N}_\vartheta\) (so that \(U^{\theta_0}\) has law \(\mu\)), the forward simulation step may replace the nonlinear solves by the tangent equation linearized at \(\hat\theta^{G}_N\) with initial law \(N(0,T(\mathbb{I}^*_{\hat\theta^{G}_N}\mathbb{I}_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})^{-1}/N)\), with plug in error \[\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathscr{C}}\Big(\operatorname{Law}\big(\sqrt N(u_\theta-u_{\hat\theta^{G}_N})\mid\theta\sim\widehat\Pi_N\big),\ \operatorname{Law}\big(U^{\hat\theta^{G}_N}\big)\Big)\le o_P(1)+C\varrho_N,\] where \(C\) depends only on the Lipschitz constant of \(\vartheta\mapsto\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\), on the Lipschitz constant of \(\vartheta\mapsto\mathcal{N}_\vartheta\) in \(\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\) from (U3), on the norm of \(\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0}:H^{-k-2}\to\mathscr{C}\) and on the first moment of \(\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\) in \(H^{-k-2}\).

Bernstein von Mises for the identity skip Gaussian head quantile generator, conditional on the uniform hypothesis

Theorem 16.10, page 285 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: conditional on Assumption U (uniform Bernstein von Mises) for the one step summary, clause (U4) included, which the book states as open (Open Question Uniformity); Assumption (U2’) on the whitened training residuals, which the book proves for localized training proposals (catalog entry whitened_residual_assumption_under_a_localized_proposal) and which fails in general for training parameters drawn from the prior when the forward map is nonlinear; and the bounds on the error of the summary (smaller order than the localization rate in L2 and tending to zero in the Sobolev norm of the localization ball), which the same open question asks for..

Statement. Setting. Data \(Y_i=u_\theta(X_i)+\varepsilon_i\), \(i\le N\), with \(X_i\) i.i.d. uniform on \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\), \(\Omega=[0,1]^d\) the torus, \(\varepsilon_i\sim N(0,I_W)\) independent, \(u_\theta\) the solution of an evolution equation with initial condition \(\theta\); \((e_j)\) the Laplace eigenbasis with eigenvalues \(\lambda_j\asymp j^{2/d}\); the prior \(\Pi\) the law of \((\sqrt N\delta_N)^{-1}\sum_j\lambda_j^{-\gamma/2}g_je_j\), \(g_j\) i.i.d. \(N(0,1)\), \(\delta_N=N^{-\gamma/(2\gamma+d)}\); \(\Pi_N\) the posterior, \(\theta_0\) the truth, \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) the law of the data \(Z^{(N)}\); \(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\) the linearization of \(\theta\mapsto u_\theta\) at \(\vartheta\) and \(\mathcal{N}_\vartheta=N(0,T(\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta)^{-1})\). Assume Conditions 1 and 2 of Nickl (2024) (as in catalog entry bvm_for_gbc) with integer \(\gamma>\max(3\zeta+3d/2+2,\eta_0)\) and \(k>\max(\zeta+5d/2,\eta_0)\), \(\zeta>d/2\) the index of Condition 2D; let \(\bar\gamma\) satisfy \(\max(2,2\zeta)<\bar\gamma<\gamma-\max(1,d/2)\) and \(\tilde\delta_N(\xi)=\delta_N^{(\bar\gamma-\xi)/(\bar\gamma+1)}\).

Summary and generator. Let \(J=J_N\asymp N\delta_N^2\), \(p_N\) the projection onto the first \(J\) modes, and \(\hat S_N\in\mathbb{R}^J\) the cross fitted one step efficient score summary: the sample is split into halves \(\mathcal{I}_1,\mathcal{I}_2\), preliminary estimates \(\bar\theta^{[b]}_N\) computed from half \(b\) satisfy \(\|\bar\theta^{[b]}_N-\theta_0\|_{H^\xi}=O_P(\tilde\delta_N(\xi))\) for \(0\le\xi\le\bar\gamma\), and \(\hat S_{N,j}=\frac12\sum_{b=1,2}\big[\langle e_j,\bar\theta^{[b]}_N\rangle+|\mathcal{I}_{3-b}|^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{3-b}}\langle\mathbb{I}_{\bar\theta^{[b]}_N}p_N(T(\mathbb{I}^*_{\bar\theta^{[b]}_N}\mathbb{I}_{\bar\theta^{[b]}_N})^{-1}e_j)(X_i),Y_i-u_{\bar\theta^{[b]}_N}(X_i)\rangle_W\big]\) for \(j\le J\). Let \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)=T(\mathcal{L}^*\mathcal{L})^{-1}\) be the information diagonal of the heat (or Stokes) solution operator \(\mathcal{L}\), \(\iota_j=(1-e^{-2\nu\lambda_jT})/(2\nu\lambda_jT)\), and write \(\Sigma=T(\mathbb{I}^*_{\theta_0}\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0})^{-1}\) restricted to the first \(J\) modes as \(\Sigma=\mathcal{D}^{1/2}(\operatorname{Id}+\Xi)\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\). From \(M\) simulated pairs \((\theta^{(m)},Z^{(m)})\) with summaries \(\hat S^{(m)}_N\) form the whitened residuals \(W^{(m)}=\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\sqrt N(\theta^{(m)}-\hat S^{(m)}_N)\in\mathbb{R}^J\) and \(\hat C_M=M^{-1}\sum_mW^{(m)}W^{(m)\top}\), and let \(\widehat G(s,U)=s+N^{-1/2}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\hat C_M^{1/2}\Phi^{-1}(U)\), \(U\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]^J\), with \(\Phi^{-1}\) the standard normal quantile function applied coordinatewise; the GBC posterior \(\widehat\Pi_N\) is the law of \(\widehat G(\hat S_N,U)\) given the data and the training sample, that is \(N(\hat S_N,N^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\hat C_M\mathcal{D}^{1/2})\).

Hypotheses. (a) Assumption U with \(S_N=\hat S_N\), clause (U4) included: there is a set \(\Theta_N\) with (U1) \(\Pi(\Theta_N)\to1\), \(\theta_0\in\Theta_N\) for all large \(N\) and \(\sup_{\vartheta\in\Theta_N}\mathbb{E}^N_\vartheta\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\big(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt N(\theta-S_N)\mid Z^{(N)}),\mathcal{N}_\vartheta\big)\to0\); (U2) these discrepancies are uniformly integrable under the prior predictive law; (U3) \(\vartheta\mapsto T(\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta)^{-1}\) is Lipschitz from \((\Theta_N,\|\cdot\|_{H^\zeta})\) into bounded operators \(H^{-k-2}\to H^{-k-4}\) and \(\vartheta\mapsto\mathcal{N}_\vartheta\) is Lipschitz from \((\Theta_N,\|\cdot\|_{H^\zeta})\) into the laws on \(H^{-k-2}\) with \(\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\), with constants free of \(N\), the first moments \(m_1(\vartheta)=\int\|x\|_{H^{-k-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{N}_\vartheta(x)\) satisfy \(\int_{\Theta_N^c}m_1\,\mathrm{d}\Pi\to0\) and \(\Pi(\Theta_N^c)\sup_{\Theta_N}m_1\to0\), \(S_N\in\Theta_N\) with probability tending to one and \(\mathbb{E}^\Pi[\|\theta-S_N\|_{H^\zeta}\mid S_N]\to0\); (U4) for every \(\theta_0\in\Theta_N\), \(\mathbb{E}\big[\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\big(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt N(\theta-S_N)\mid Z^{(N)}),\mathcal{N}_{\theta_0}\big)\mid S_N\big]\to0\) in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability. (b) Assumption (U2’): \(W^{(m)}=W^{(m)}_{g}+W^{(m)}_{r}\) with the pairs \((W^{(m)}_{g},W^{(m)}_{r})\) i.i.d. given \(Z^{(N)}\), \(\varkappa_N=\sup_{\|v\|_2=1}\|\langle v,W^{(m)}_{g}\rangle\|_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}=O_P(1)\) under \(P^N_{\theta_0}\), where \(\|\chi\|_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}=\inf\{K>0:\mathbb{E}[e^{\chi^2/K^2}\mid Z^{(N)}]\le2\}\), and, in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability, \(\mathbb{E}[\|W^{(m)}_{r}\|_2^2\mid Z^{(N)}]\to0\) and \(\|\mathbb{E}[W^{(m)}_{g}W^{(m)\top}_{g}\mid Z^{(N)}]-(\operatorname{Id}+\Xi)\|_{op}\to0\). (c) The hypotheses of the equivalence of the one step summary with the efficient centering (catalog entry equivalence_with_the_efficient_centering), its two conditions on the exponents included: \(\sqrt N\,\tilde\delta_N(0)\tilde\delta_N(\zeta)\to0\), \(\gamma<(\bar\gamma+1)(\kappa-d/2)\) for some \(2d+\zeta<\kappa<k-d/2\) unless \(\mathbb{I}^*_{\theta_0}\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0}\) is diagonal in \((e_j)\) or the preliminary estimates converge at the rate \(\delta_N\) in \(H^{-1}\), and, for every \(B>0\), a constant \(L(B)\) with \(\|\mathbb{I}_\vartheta p_N(T(\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta)^{-1}e_j)-\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0}p_N(T(\mathbb{I}^*_{\theta_0}\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0})^{-1}e_j)\|_{L^2(\mathcal{X})}\le L(B)\|\vartheta-\theta_0\|_{H^\zeta}\|e_j\|_{H^{\kappa+2}}\) for all \(\vartheta\) with \(\|\vartheta\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}\le B\) and all \(j\le J_N\). (d) \(\|\hat S_N-\theta_0\|_{L^2}=o_P(\tilde\delta_N(0))\) and \(\|\hat S_N-\theta_0\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}=o_P(1)\).

Conclusion. If \(M=M_N\) satisfies \(J_N/M_N\to0\), then \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}(\widehat\Pi_N,\Pi_N)\to0\) in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability (condition (G1) of the transfer theorem) and condition (G2) of that theorem holds (mass at most \(e^{-bN\delta_N^2}\) off the localization set and \(\mathbb{E}^{\widehat\Pi_N}\|\theta\|^q_{H^{\bar\gamma}}=O_P(1)\)). Consequently the conclusions of the transfer theorem (catalog entry bvm_for_gbc) hold for the GBC posterior produced by this generator, those of its parts (ii) to (iv) under the additional hypotheses stated there.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3b.tex, label m:thm:qnnA.

No uniform guarantee: minimax lower bound for conditional quantile generators over monotone Hölder classes

Theorem 16.15, page 290 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. For \(\beta>0\) and \(J\in\mathbb{N}\) let \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) be the set of maps \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) from \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\) to \(\mathbb{R}\) such that (i) \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}\le1\), where, with \(\ell\) the largest integer strictly below \(\beta\), \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=\ell}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-\ell}\), the maxima running over multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\); (ii) \(\tau\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) is nondecreasing for every \(s\). Let \(P_{Q,s}=Q(\cdot\mid s)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\) be the law that \(Q\) encodes at \(s\) and \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\) the Wasserstein distance of order one on the line. The training sample consists of \(M\) i.i.d. copies of \((S,\theta)\) with \(S\sim\mu\) and \(\theta\mid S\sim P_{Q,S}\), where the design law \(\mu\) on \([0,1]^J\) has a Lebesgue density bounded above by \(C_\mu\) and below by \(c_\mu>0\) (so \(C_\mu\ge1\)). A generator is any measurable, possibly randomized, map \(\hat P_M\) from the training sample and a summary value \(s\) to a probability measure \(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s)\) on \(\mathbb{R}\).

Let \(\beta>0\), \(J\ge1\), and let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary. There is a constant \(c=c(\beta)>0\), depending only on \(\beta\) and on two fixed profile functions, and in particular not on \(J\), such that for every \(M\ge1\) and every generator \(\hat P_M\), \[\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)}P\Big(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\big(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0),P_{Q,s_0}\big)\ge c\,(C_\mu M)^{-\beta/(2\beta+J)}\Big)\ge\frac18,\] the probability being over the \(M\) training pairs, and consequently \[\inf_{\hat P_M}\ \sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)}\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\big(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0),P_{Q,s_0}\big)\ge\frac c8\,(C_\mu M)^{-\beta/(2\beta+J)}.\] Consequently, if a GBC generator trained on \(M_N\) pairs is required to satisfy the accuracy condition (G1) of the transfer theorem at the summary value \(s_0\) uniformly over \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J_N)\), that is, if \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}(\hat P_{M_N}(\cdot\mid s_0),P_{Q,s_0})\to0\) in probability is to hold for every conditional quantile map in the class (reading this as (G1), which compares with the posterior given all the data, uses the asymptotic sufficiency of the summary and so Assumption U of the book), then necessarily \(\sqrt N\,(C_\mu M_N)^{-\beta/(2\beta+J_N)}\to0\), hence \[M_N\ge C_\mu^{-1}N^{(2\beta+J_N)/(2\beta)}=C_\mu^{-1}N^{1+J_N/(2\beta)}\qquad\text{for all large }N,\] which grows faster than any polynomial in \(N\) as soon as \(J_N\to\infty\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3b.tex, label m:thm:qnnB.

The minimax exponent for conditional quantile generators is attained for Hölder smoothness at most one (histogram generator)

Proposition 16.16, page 293 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. For \(\beta>0\) and \(J\in\mathbb{N}\) let \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) be the set of maps \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) from \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\) to \(\mathbb{R}\) with \(C^\beta\) norm at most one (for \(\beta\le1\), \(\|Q\|_\infty+\sup_{z\ne z'}|Q(z)-Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^\beta\le1\) on \([0,1]^{1+J}\)) that are nondecreasing in \(\tau\) for every \(s\); let \(P_{Q,s}=Q(\cdot\mid s)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\), let \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\) be the Wasserstein distance of order one on the line, and let the training pairs \((S^{(i)},\theta^{(i)})\), \(i\le M\), be i.i.d. with \(S\sim\mu\) and \(\theta\mid S\sim P_{Q,S}\), where \(\mu\) has a Lebesgue density on \([0,1]^J\) bounded below by \(c_\mu>0\) and above by \(C_\mu\).

Let \(\beta\in(0,1]\), partition \([0,1]^J\) into \(m^J\) cubes of side \(h=1/m\) with \(m=\max\{1,\lfloor M^{1/(2\beta+J)}\rfloor\}\), and let \(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s)\) be the empirical law of \(\{\theta^{(i)}:S^{(i)}\in B(s)\}\), where \(B(s)\) is the cube containing \(s\), and any fixed law on \([-1,1]\) if that cube contains no \(S^{(i)}\). Then \[\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)}\ \sup_{s\in[0,1]^J}\ \mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\big(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s),P_{Q,s}\big)\le C(\beta,c_\mu)\,J^{\beta/2}\,M^{-\beta/(2\beta+J)},\] with \(C(\beta,c_\mu)\) depending only on \(\beta\) and \(c_\mu\). In particular, at fixed \(\beta\) and \(J\) a single explicit estimator has risk \(O(M^{-\beta/(2\beta+J)})=o(M^{-\beta/(2\beta+J+1)})\), so no lower bound with the exponent \(\beta/(2\beta+J+1)\) and a positive constant can hold: it is the summary dimension, and not the summary dimension plus one, that governs the problem.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3b.tex, label m:prop:qnnUB.

The minimax exponent for conditional quantile generators is attained for smoothness between one and two by jittered local linear quantile regression

Proposition 16.19, page 295 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. For \(\beta>0\) and \(J\in\mathbb{N}\) let \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) be the set of maps \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) from \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\) to \(\mathbb{R}\) with \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}\le1\), where, with \(\ell\) the largest integer strictly below \(\beta\), \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=\ell}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-\ell}\) (maxima over multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\)), and with \(\tau\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) nondecreasing for every \(s\). Let \(P_{Q,s}=Q(\cdot\mid s)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\), \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\) the Wasserstein distance of order one on the line, and let the training pairs \((S^{(i)},\theta^{(i)})\), \(i\le M\), be i.i.d. with \(S\sim\mu\) and \(\theta\mid S\sim P_{Q,S}\), where \(\mu\) has a Lebesgue density on \([0,1]^J\) between \(c_\mu>0\) and \(C_\mu\). Let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary.

Let \(\beta\in(1,2]\) and \(J\ge1\). Put \(h=\min\{1/8,M^{-1/(2\beta+J)}\}\), \(I=\{i\le M:\|S^{(i)}-s_0\|_2\le h\}\), and for \(i\in I\) let \(X_i=(S^{(i)}-s_0)/h\) and \(\tilde\theta_i=\theta^{(i)}+h^\beta V_i\), where \(V_1,\dots,V_M\) are i.i.d. uniform on \([0,1]\) and independent of the training pairs. For \(\tau\in(0,1)\) let \(\rho_\tau(r)=r(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{r<0\})\), and let \((\hat a_\tau,\hat b_\tau)\) be the minimizer of least Euclidean norm of \((a,b)\mapsto\sum_{i\in I}\rho_\tau(\tilde\theta_i-a-b\cdot X_i)\) over \(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^J\) when the vectors \((1,X_i)\), \(i\in I\), span \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\), and \((\hat a_\tau,\hat b_\tau)=0\) otherwise. Let \(\hat Q(\tau)=\max\{-1,\min\{1,\hat a_\tau\}\}\) and \(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0)=\hat Q_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\). There is a constant \(C\) depending only on \(\beta\), \(J\), \(c_\mu\) and \(C_\mu\) such that for every \(M\ge1\) \[\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)}\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\big(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0),P_{Q,s_0}\big)\le C\,M^{-\beta/(2\beta+J)}.\] In particular, for every \(\beta\le2\), at fixed \(\beta\) and \(J\) no lower bound with the exponent \(\beta/(2\beta+J+1)\) and a positive constant can hold.

A local linear quantile fit is nearly monotone in its error (deterministic lemma for monotone Hölder quantile maps)

Lemma 16.21, page 296 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Let \(\beta\in(1,2]\), \(J\ge1\), and let \(Q:[0,1]\times[0,1]^J\to\mathbb{R}\), \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\), satisfy \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le1}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=1}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-1}\le1\) (multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\)) and be nondecreasing in \(\tau\) for every \(s\). Let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary, \(h\in(0,1/8]\), \(\bar B=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^J:\|x\|_2\le1\}\), \(C_G=2\max\{(2\sqrt J)^{1/\beta},2^{3+1/\beta}\}\), \(K=2^{\beta-1}C_G^\beta\) and \(\eta=(K+\sqrt J+1)h^\beta\). For \(x\in\bar B\) let \(F_x\) be the distribution function of \(P_{Q,s_0+hx}=Q(\cdot\mid s_0+hx)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\) and \[\tilde F_x(y)=h^{-\beta}\int_{y-h^\beta}^{y}F_x(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,\] the distribution function of \(\theta+h^\beta V\) for \(\theta\sim P_{Q,s_0+hx}\) and an independent \(V\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\); for \(\tau\in(0,1)\) let \(T_\tau(x)=Q(\tau\mid s_0)+h\nabla_sQ(\tau\mid s_0)\cdot x\). Let \(\tau\in(0,1)\), let \(p:\mathbb{R}^J\to\mathbb{R}\) be affine, and put \(w=p-T_\tau\) and \(\delta(x)=\tilde F_x(p(x))-\tau\). Then for all \(x,x'\in\bar B\) and \(t\ge0\): (i) if \(\delta(x)>0\) then \(w(x)\ge-\eta\), and if \(\delta(x)<0\) then \(w(x)\le\eta\); (ii) if \(w(x')\le t\) and \(w(x)>3t+4\eta\) then \(\delta(x')\le\delta(x)\), and if \(w(x')\ge-t\) and \(w(x)<-3t-4\eta\) then \(\delta(x)\le\delta(x')\); (iii) if \(w(x)>\eta\) then \(\delta(x)\ge\min\{\frac23(w(x)-\eta),1-\tau\}\), and if \(w(x)<-\eta\) then \(-\delta(x)\ge\min\{\frac23(-w(x)-\eta),\tau\}\).

Moment conditions force a small local linear quantile fit

Lemma 16.22, page 298 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Let \(\beta\in(1,2]\), \(J\ge1\), and let \(Q:[0,1]\times[0,1]^J\to\mathbb{R}\), \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\), satisfy \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le1}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=1}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-1}\le1\) (multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\)) and be nondecreasing in \(\tau\) for every \(s\). Let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary, \(h\in(0,1/8]\), \(\bar B=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^J:\|x\|_2\le1\}\), \(C_G=2\max\{(2\sqrt J)^{1/\beta},2^{3+1/\beta}\}\), \(K=2^{\beta-1}C_G^\beta\) and \(\eta=(K+\sqrt J+1)h^\beta\). For \(x\in\bar B\) let \(F_x\) be the distribution function of \(Q(\cdot\mid s_0+hx)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\) and \(\tilde F_x(y)=h^{-\beta}\int_{y-h^\beta}^yF_x(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\). Let \(\tau\in(0,1)\), \(T_\tau(x)=Q(\tau\mid s_0)+h\nabla_sQ(\tau\mid s_0)\cdot x\), \(p:\mathbb{R}^J\to\mathbb{R}\) affine, \(w=p-T_\tau\) and \(\delta(x)=\tilde F_x(p(x))-\tau\).

Let \(g\) be a probability density on \(\bar B\) with \(g\le g_+\), let \(\omega_{J-1}\) be the volume of the unit ball of \(\mathbb{R}^{J-1}\) (with \(\omega_0=1\)), and put \(C_R=1+\frac{20}{3}g_+\omega_{J-1}\) and \(c_B=12C_R(1+\sqrt J)\). Let \(\kappa\ge0\) be such that \(|\int\delta g|\le\kappa\) and \(|\int\delta(x)x_kg(x)\,\mathrm{d}x|\le\kappa\) for \(k=1,\dots,J\). If \(\min\{\tau,1-\tau\}>5c_B\kappa\), then \[\sup_{x\in\bar B}|w(x)|\le32\,C_R\,\eta+20\,C_R\,c_B\,\kappa.\]

The minimax exponent for conditional quantile generators is attained for smoothness above two when the conditional densities are bounded

Proposition 16.25, page 302 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. For \(\beta>0\) and \(J\in\mathbb{N}\) let \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) be the set of maps \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) from \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\) to \(\mathbb{R}\) with \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}\le1\), where, with \(\ell\) the largest integer strictly below \(\beta\), \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=\ell}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-\ell}\) (maxima over multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\)), and with \(\tau\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) nondecreasing for every \(s\). For \(\beta>1\) and \(\underline c\in(0,1]\) let \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J;\underline c)\) be the set of \(Q\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) with \(\partial_\tau Q\ge\underline c\) on \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\), so that every \(P_{Q,s}=Q(\cdot\mid s)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\) has a density between \(1\) and \(1/\underline c\) on its support and no atom. Let \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\) be the Wasserstein distance of order one on the line, let the training pairs \((S^{(i)},\theta^{(i)})\), \(i\le M\), be i.i.d. with \(S\sim\mu\) and \(\theta\mid S\sim P_{Q,S}\), where \(\mu\) has a Lebesgue density on \([0,1]^J\) between \(c_\mu>0\) and \(C_\mu\), and let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary.

Let \(\beta>2\), \(J\ge1\) and \(\underline c\in(0,1]\). Let \(L_\ell\) be the number of multi indices \(\alpha\) on \(\mathbb{R}^J\) with \(|\alpha|\le\ell\) and \(\Psi(x)=(x^\alpha)_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{L_\ell}\). Put \(h=\min\{1/8,M^{-1/(2\beta+J)}\}\), \(I=\{i\le M:\|S^{(i)}-s_0\|_2\le h\}\) and \(X_i=(S^{(i)}-s_0)/h\) for \(i\in I\). For \(\tau\in(0,1)\) let \(\rho_\tau(r)=r(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{r<0\})\) and let \(\hat c_\tau\) be the minimizer of least Euclidean norm of \(c\mapsto\sum_{i\in I}\rho_\tau(\theta^{(i)}-c\cdot\Psi(X_i))\) over \(\mathbb{R}^{L_\ell}\) when the vectors \(\Psi(X_i)\), \(i\in I\), span \(\mathbb{R}^{L_\ell}\), and \(\hat c_\tau=0\) otherwise. Let \(\hat Q(\tau)=\max\{-1,\min\{1,\hat c_\tau\cdot\Psi(0)\}\}\) and \(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0)=\hat Q_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\). There is a constant \(C\) depending only on \(\beta\), \(J\), \(c_\mu\), \(C_\mu\) and \(\underline c\) such that for every \(M\ge1\) \[\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J;\underline c)}\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\big(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0),P_{Q,s_0}\big)\le C\,M^{-\beta/(2\beta+J)}.\] The two maps in the proof of the lower bound (catalog entry no_uniform_guarantee_for_quantile_generators) have \(\partial_\tau Q\ge\frac18\), so with that theorem and the case \(1<\beta\le2\) (catalog entry quantile_generator_exponent_attained_for_beta_between_one_and_two) the exponent \(\beta/(2\beta+J)\) is the minimax exponent over \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J;\underline c)\) at an interior point for every \(\beta>1\) when \(\underline c\le\frac18\).

A local polynomial quantile fit is uniformly small when the conditional densities are bounded

Lemma 16.26, page 303 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. For \(\beta>1\), \(J\ge1\) and \(\underline c\in(0,1]\) let \(\ell\) be the largest integer strictly below \(\beta\) and let \(Q:[0,1]\times[0,1]^J\to\mathbb{R}\), \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\), satisfy \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}\le1\), where \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=\ell}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-\ell}\) (maxima over multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\)), be nondecreasing in \(\tau\) for every \(s\), and satisfy \(\partial_\tau Q\ge\underline c\) on \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\). Let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary, \(h\in(0,1/8]\), \(\tau\in(0,1)\), \(\bar B=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^J:\|x\|_2\le1\}\) with volume \(\omega_J\), \(F_x\) the distribution function of \(Q(\cdot\mid s_0+hx)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\) for \(x\in\bar B\), and \(T^\ell_\tau(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\partial_s^\alpha Q(\tau\mid s_0)\,h^{|\alpha|}x^\alpha/\alpha!\), the Taylor polynomial of degree \(\ell\) in \(s\) written in the rescaled variable. Let \(L_\ell\) be the number of multi indices \(\alpha\) on \(\mathbb{R}^J\) with \(|\alpha|\le\ell\), \(\Psi(x)=(x^\alpha)_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\), \(c_\ell\in(0,1]\) the largest number with \(\int_{\bar B}u^2\ge c_\ell\,\omega_J\sup_{\bar B}u^2\) for every polynomial \(u\) of degree at most \(\ell\), \(K_\ell\) the smallest number with \(\max_\alpha|c_\alpha|\le K_\ell\sup_{\bar B}|c\cdot\Psi|\) for every \(c\in\mathbb{R}^{L_\ell}\), and \(K_T=J^{\ell/2}/\ell!\).

Let \(p\) be a polynomial of degree at most \(\ell\), \(w=p-T^\ell_\tau\) and \(\delta(x)=F_x(p(x))-\tau\). Let \(g\) be a probability density on \(\bar B\) with \(g\ge g_->0\), and let \(\kappa\ge0\) be such that \(|\int\delta(x)x^\alpha g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x|\le\kappa\) for \(|\alpha|\le\ell\). Put \[c_7=(g_-\omega_Jc_\ell)^{1/2},\qquad\bar\kappa=L_\ell K_\ell\kappa+6K_Th^\beta/\underline c.\] If \(\min\{\tau,1-\tau\}>2\sqrt2\,\bar\kappa/c_7^3\), then \(\sup_{\bar B}|w|\le8\bar\kappa/c_7^4\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3b.tex, label m:lem:qpoly.

No two point lower bound gives a smaller exponent for conditional quantile generators

Proposition 16.27, page 304 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. For \(\beta>0\) and \(J\in\mathbb{N}\) let \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) be the set of maps \((\tau,s)\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) from \([0,1]\times[0,1]^J\) to \(\mathbb{R}\) with \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}\le1\), where, with \(\ell\) the largest integer strictly below \(\beta\), \(\|Q\|_{C^\beta}=\max_{|\alpha|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha Q\|_\infty+\max_{|\alpha|=\ell}\sup_{z\ne z'}|\partial^\alpha Q(z)-\partial^\alpha Q(z')|/\|z-z'\|_2^{\beta-\ell}\) (maxima over multi indices on \(\mathbb{R}^{1+J}\)), and with \(\tau\mapsto Q(\tau\mid s)\) nondecreasing for every \(s\). Let \(P_{Q,s}=Q(\cdot\mid s)_\#\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]\), \(\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}\) the Wasserstein distance of order one on the line, and let a training pair be \((S,\theta)\) with \(S\sim\mu\) and \(\theta\mid S\sim P_{Q,S}\), where \(\mu\) has a Lebesgue density on \([0,1]^J\) between \(c_\mu>0\) and \(C_\mu\). Let \(s_0\in[0,1]^J\) be at Euclidean distance at least \(1/8\) from the boundary.

Let \(\beta>0\) and \(J\ge1\). There is a constant \(c_8>0\) depending only on \(\beta\) and \(J\) such that for all \(Q_0,Q_1\in\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\), with \(P^{(i)}\) the joint law of one pair under \(Q_i\) and \(\Delta=\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}(P_{Q_0,s_0},P_{Q_1,s_0})\), the squared Hellinger distance satisfies \[H^2\big(P^{(1)},P^{(0)}\big)\ge c_8\,c_\mu\,\Delta^{2+J/\beta}.\] Consequently, for every such pair and every \(M\ge1\) some generator \(\hat P_M\) trained on \(M\) i.i.d. pairs has \(\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{W}_{1,\mathbb{R}}(\hat P_M(\cdot\mid s_0),P_{Q_i,s_0})\le\{2/(c_8c_\mu M)\}^{\beta/(2\beta+J)}\) for \(i=0\) and \(i=1\), so that no lower bound drawn from two maps in \(\mathcal{Q}_\beta(J)\) has an exponent smaller than \(\beta/(2\beta+J)\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3b.tex, label m:prop:qnntwo.

Posterior contraction for the initial condition of a PDE under a sparse deep ReLU network (Polson Ročková) prior

Theorem 16.28, page 306 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Consider data \(Z^{(N)}=(Y_i,X_i)_{i\le N}\) with \(Y_i=\mathscr{G}(\theta)(X_i)+\varepsilon_i\), \(X_i\) i.i.d. uniform on \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^d\) and \(\varepsilon_i\sim N(0,I_W)\) i.i.d. and independent of them, where \(\mathscr{G}(\theta)\) is the solution of an evolution equation with initial condition \(\theta:\mathbb{T}^d\to\mathbb{R}\), and let \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) be the law of the data under \(\theta_0\). Let \(\Pi\) be the Polson Ročková spike and slab prior on deep ReLU networks \(\theta:\mathbb{T}^d\to\mathbb{R}\) with their width and sparsity hyperpriors and a depth of order \(\log N\), let \(\Theta_N\) be the associated sieve, let \(p\) be the input dimension of the networks in \(\Theta_N\) (\(p=d\) when networks on \([0,1]^d\) approximate the restriction of \(\theta_0\) to \([0,1]^d\), or \(p=2d\) when every network is composed with the periodic embedding \(x\mapsto(\cos2\pi x_1,\sin2\pi x_1,\dots,\cos2\pi x_d,\sin2\pi x_d)\)), and set \[B_N:=1+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_N}\big(\|\theta\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\operatorname{Lip}(\theta)\big),\] so that \(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_N}\|\theta\|_{H^1(\mathbb{T}^d)}\lesssim B_N\). Let \(\theta_0\in C^\alpha(\mathbb{T}^d)\) with \(d/2+1<\alpha<p\). Suppose the forward map satisfies Conditions 2A, 2B and 2E of Nickl (2024) on \(L^\infty\) balls (uniform \(L^\infty\) bounds, \(L^2\) Lipschitz continuity, and the stability estimate \(\|\mathscr{G}(\theta)-\mathscr{G}(\theta_0)\|_{L^2(\mathcal{X})}\ge c\|\theta-\theta_0\|_{H^{-1}}\)). Then with \(\epsilon_N=N^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\log^\delta N\), \(\delta>1\), there are \(L,L'>0\) with \[\Pi\big(\theta:\|\mathscr{G}(\theta)-\mathscr{G}(\theta_0)\|_{L^2(\mathcal{X})}\le L\epsilon_N\mid Z^{(N)}\big)\to1,\qquad\Pi\big(\theta:\|\theta-\theta_0\|_{H^{-1}}\le L\epsilon_N\mid Z^{(N)}\big)\to1,\] in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability, and, by interpolation between \(H^{-1}\) and \(H^1\) along the sieve, \[\Pi\big(\theta:\|\theta-\theta_0\|_{L^2}\le L'(B_N\epsilon_N)^{1/2}\mid Z^{(N)}\big)\to1\] in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability. The third statement is a rate only when \(B_N\epsilon_N\to0\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:thm:PR.

Exactness of the one step summary in the linear Gaussian model

Lemma 16.32, page 310 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. In the linear model \(Y=\Phi\theta+\varepsilon\), \(\varepsilon\sim N(0,\varsigma^2\operatorname{Id}_N)\), with prior \(\theta\sim N(0,\Sigma_0)\), let \(F=\Phi^\top\Phi/\varsigma^2\) and \(\Sigma_N=(F+\Sigma_0^{-1})^{-1}\), and let the one step summary be evaluated at any point \(\bar\theta\): \[S(Y)=\bar\theta+\Sigma_N\Big(\Phi^\top\big(Y-\Phi\bar\theta\big)/\varsigma^2-\Sigma_0^{-1}\bar\theta\Big).\] Then \(S(Y)=\Sigma_N\big(F\theta+\Phi^\top\varepsilon/\varsigma^2\big)\) identically in \(\bar\theta\): the summary is exactly the posterior mean, and in particular does not depend on the linearization point.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:lem:exact.

Residual law under a localized training proposal: prior fraction mean and variance inflation

Theorem 16.33, page 310 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. In the linear model \(Y=\Phi\theta+\varepsilon\), \(\varepsilon\sim N(0,\varsigma^2\operatorname{Id}_N)\), with prior \(N(0,\Sigma_0)\) on \(D\) retained modes, let \(F=\Phi^\top\Phi/\varsigma^2\), \(\Sigma_N=(F+\Sigma_0^{-1})^{-1}\), and for a point \(\bar\theta\) let \(S(Y)=\bar\theta+\Sigma_N(\Phi^\top(Y-\Phi\bar\theta)/\varsigma^2-\Sigma_0^{-1}\bar\theta)\) (which equals the posterior mean). Let the training parameter be drawn from \(q_c=N(\bar\theta,c^2\Sigma_N)\), independent of \(\varepsilon\), so that \(c\) is the localization radius in posterior standard deviations, let \(R=\theta-S(Y)\), and let \(P=\Sigma_N\Sigma_0^{-1}\) be the prior fraction operator, with eigenvalues \(p_1,\dots,p_D\), and \(s_j=1-p_j\). Then \[\mathbb{E}_{q_c}(R)=P\bar\theta,\qquad\operatorname{Var}_{q_c}(R)=c^2P\Sigma_NP^\top+\Sigma_NF\Sigma_N,\] both without further hypotheses. If in addition \(\Sigma_0\) and \(\Phi^\top\Phi\) commute, so that one orthonormal basis diagonalizes \(P\), \(\Sigma_N\) and \(F\) together, then in that basis \(\operatorname{Var}_{q_c}(R)_j=\Sigma_{N,j}(s_j+c^2p_j^2)\). Consequently: (i) under the prior itself, \(\mathbb{E}(R)=0\) and \(\operatorname{Var}(R)=\Sigma_N\), so the identity head is exact; (ii) under \(q_c\) the residual has the mean \(P\bar\theta\), the prior shrinkage of the proposal center, which does not depend on \(c\) and is of first order in the prior fraction; a head that adds the sample mean of the residuals to the center therefore displaces the GBC posterior by \(P\bar\theta\), whereas a head that centers the residuals, or subtracts \(P\bar\theta\) analytically, does not; (iii) with the mean removed, the head misstates the posterior standard deviation in direction \(j\) by the factor \(\sqrt{s_j+c^2p_j^2}\), so the relative error in posterior standard deviations is \[\beta(c)=\frac1D\sum_{j\le D}\Big|\sqrt{s_j+c^2p_j^2}-1\Big|,\] which is \(O(\max_jp_j)\) as \(c\to0\) and grows like \(c\,\frac1D\sum_jp_j\) for large \(c\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:thm:locbias.

The localized GBC design (center and rescale) meets the accuracy conditions (G1) and (G2) in the linear Gaussian model

Corollary 16.37, page 315 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. In the linear model \(Y=\Phi\theta+\varepsilon\), \(\varepsilon\sim N(0,\varsigma^2\operatorname{Id}_N)\), with prior \(N(0,\Sigma_0)\), let \(F=\Phi^\top\Phi/\varsigma^2\), \(\Sigma_N=(F+\Sigma_0^{-1})^{-1}\), and suppose \(\Sigma_0\) and \(\Phi^\top\Phi\) commute; let \(P=\Sigma_N\Sigma_0^{-1}\), with eigenvalues \(p_j\), \(s_j=1-p_j\), and for a preliminary estimate \(\bar\theta_N\) let \(S(Y)=\bar\theta_N+\Sigma_N(\Phi^\top(Y-\Phi\bar\theta_N)/\varsigma^2-\Sigma_0^{-1}\bar\theta_N)\), the posterior mean, so that the exact posterior is \(\Pi_N=N(S(Y),\Sigma_N)\). Consider the localized GBC generator: training parameters \(\theta^{(m)}\sim q_c=N(\bar\theta_N,c^2\Sigma_N)\), \(m\le M\), with simulated data \(Y^{(m)}\); an identity head with the residuals \(\theta^{(m)}-S(Y^{(m)})\) centered at their sample mean, whitened by the sample covariance of the centered residuals rescaled by \((s_j+c^2p_j^2)^{-1/2}\) in the eigenbasis of \(P\), and centered at \(S(Y)\) for the observed data, with no residual mean added to the center, no affine head and no importance weighting back to the prior; let \(\widehat\Pi_N\) be the law it produces. Then: (i) (exactness in population) for every \(c>0\) and every center \(\bar\theta_N\), the law the head produces at the observed data is exactly the posterior \(N(S(Y),\Sigma_N)\): localization contributes no bias at all; (ii) (finite \(M\)) with \(M=M_N\) simulations and \(J=J_N\) retained modes, the rescaled sample covariance of the centered residuals equals \(\Sigma_N^{1/2}(\operatorname{Id}+\Delta_M)\Sigma_N^{1/2}\), where \(\|\Delta_M\|_{op}=O_P(\sqrt{J_N/M_N}+J_N/M_N)\) does not depend on \(c\); hence, when \(\Sigma_{N,j}\lesssim\lambda_j/N\) on the retained modes (\(\lambda_j\) the Laplace eigenvalues), as for the heat and Stokes equations, and \(k>d/2\), \(\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}(\widehat\Pi_N,\Pi_N)=O_P\big(N^{-1/2}(\sqrt{J_N/M_N}+J_N/M_N)\big)\), the distance being the Wasserstein distance of order one for the norm \(\|x\|^2=\sum_j\lambda_j^{-k-2}x_j^2\) on the coefficients in the first \(J\) Laplace modes, so the accuracy condition (G1) \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}(\widehat\Pi_N,\Pi_N)\to0\) holds whenever \(J_N/M_N\to0\), at every radius, and the condition (G2) (exponentially small mass off the localization set and moments of every order) holds whenever the exact posterior \(N(S(Y),\Sigma_N)\) satisfies its first clause; (iii) consequently the transfer theorem (catalog entry bvm_for_gbc) applies verbatim to this algorithm, its accuracy assumption being discharged.

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:cor:locG.

Gram matrix of the whitened weights of the one step training summary

Lemma 16.41, page 319 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Let \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\) with \(X\) uniform on \(\mathcal{X}\), let \((e_j)\) be the Laplace eigenbasis of \(\Omega=[0,1]^d\) (the torus), \(P_J\) the orthogonal projection onto \(\operatorname{span}\{e_j:j\le J\}\cong\mathbb{R}^J\) and \(Q_J=\operatorname{Id}-P_J\). Let \(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}\) be the linearization at a point \(\bar\vartheta\) of the solution map \(\theta\mapsto u_\theta\), with values in \(\mathbb{R}^{d_W}\) valued functions on \(\mathcal{X}\), let \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\) be the heat (or Stokes) information diagonal, and define \(K'_{\bar\vartheta}\) by \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\bar\vartheta}=T^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathbb{I}^*_{\bar\vartheta}\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\), that is \(\langle f,(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\bar\vartheta})g\rangle=\mathbb{E}\langle(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}f)(X),(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}g)(X)\rangle_W\). Let \(\mathcal{A}=\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\bar\vartheta}\) be bounded on \(\ell^2\) with \(\mathcal{A}\succeq a\operatorname{Id}\) for some \(a>0\), and write \(B_J=P_J\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_J\). Let the whitened weights be the \(J\times d_W\) matrix \(\phi(x)\) defined by \(\phi(x)^\top v=(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}P_J\mathcal{A}^{-1}v)(x)\) for \(v\in\mathbb{R}^J\) (equivalently, row \(j\) is \(\iota_j^{1/2}\) times the weight function \(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}P_J(T(\mathbb{I}^*_{\bar\vartheta}\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta})^{-1}e_j)\) of the one step summary linearized at \(\bar\vartheta\)), and \(G_\phi=\mathbb{E}\,\phi(X)\phi(X)^\top\) their Gram matrix. Then \[G_\phi=B_J+B_JP_J\mathcal{A}Q_J(Q_J\mathcal{A}Q_J)^{-1}Q_J\mathcal{A}P_JB_J,\] so that \(G_\phi\succeq B_J\succeq\|\mathcal{A}\|^{-1}_{op}\operatorname{Id}\) and \(\|G_\phi-B_J\|_{op}\le a^{-3}\|K'_{\bar\vartheta}Q_J\|^2_{op}\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:lem:u2pgram.

No sub Gaussian envelope uniform in the dimension for the whole whitened training residual with fresh designs

Proposition 16.42, page 319 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Let \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\), \(\Omega=[0,1]^d\) the torus, with \(X\) uniform on \(\mathcal{X}\), and let \(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\) be the linearization at \(\vartheta\) of the solution map \(\theta\mapsto u_\theta\), whose values are \(\mathbb{R}^{d_W}\) valued functions on \(\mathcal{X}\). Let \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\) be the heat (or Stokes) information diagonal, \(\iota_j=(1-e^{-2\nu\lambda_jT})/(2\nu\lambda_jT)\) with \(\lambda_j\) the Laplace eigenvalues, \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_\vartheta=T^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\), and \(P_J\) the projection onto the first \(J\) eigenfunctions \(e_1,\dots,e_J\). Fix a linearization point \(\bar\vartheta\) determined by the data \(Z^{(N)}\) at which \(\mathcal{A}=\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\bar\vartheta}\) is bounded and invertible, let \(\phi(x)\) be the \(J\times d_W\) matrix with \(\phi(x)^\top v=(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\vartheta}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}P_J\mathcal{A}^{-1}v)(x)\) for \(v\in\mathbb{R}^J\) (rows \(\phi_j(x)^\top\)), \(G_\phi=\mathbb{E}\,\phi(X)\phi(X)^\top\) and \(b_J=\operatorname{ess\,sup}_x\|\phi(x)\|^2_{op}\). Let each simulated data set \(Z^{(m)}\) be drawn at a training parameter \(\theta^{(m)}\) with its own design points \(X^{(m)}_i\), i.i.d. uniform on \(\mathcal{X}\), and its own noise \(\varepsilon^{(m)}_i\sim N(0,I_W)\), independent of each other and of \(\theta^{(m)}\), let the training summary be the one step summary linearized once at \(\bar\vartheta\) with the population information and no prior term, \(\hat S^{(m)}_{N,j}=\langle e_j,\bar\vartheta\rangle+N^{-1}\sum_{i\le N}\langle\iota_j^{-1/2}\phi_j(X^{(m)}_i),Y^{(m)}_i-u_{\bar\vartheta}(X^{(m)}_i)\rangle_W\) for \(j\le J\), and let \(W^{(m)}=\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\sqrt N(P_J\theta^{(m)}-\hat S^{(m)}_N)\) be the whitened residual. Let the training parameters have any law given \(Z^{(N)}\), and write \(\|\chi\|_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}=\inf\{K>0:\mathbb{E}[e^{\chi^2/K^2}\mid Z^{(N)}]\le2\}\). Then for every \(N\), every \(J\) and every unit vector \(v\in\mathbb{R}^J\) \[\big\|\langle v,W^{(m)}\rangle\big\|^2_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}\ge2\operatorname{ess\,sup}_x\|\phi(x)^\top v\|^2_W,\qquad\sup_{\|u\|_2=1}\big\|\langle u,W^{(m)}\rangle\big\|^2_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}\ge2b_J\ge\frac{2\,\mathrm{tr}\,G_\phi}{d_W}.\] If \(\mathcal{A}\) is bounded and \(\mathcal{A}\succeq a\operatorname{Id}\) for some \(a>0\), then \(\mathrm{tr}\,G_\phi\ge J\|\mathcal{A}\|^{-1}_{op}\). For the heat and Stokes equations, observed from time zero, \(G_\phi=\operatorname{Id}_J\) and \(\|\langle e_J,W^{(m)}\rangle\|^2_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}\ge2\|e_J\|^2_{L^\infty}/\iota_J\ge2/\iota_J\), which grows like \(\lambda_J\); for the real trigonometric basis the bound is \(4/\iota_J\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:prop:u2pfalse.

The whitened training residual assumption (U2’) holds under a localized training proposal

Theorem 16.43, page 320 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Setting. Data \(Z^{(N)}\): \(Y_i=u_\theta(X_i)+\varepsilon_i\), \(i\le N\), \(X_i\) i.i.d. uniform on \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\), \(\Omega=[0,1]^d\) the torus, \(\varepsilon_i\sim N(0,I_W)\) in \(\mathbb{R}^{d_W}\); \((e_j)\) the Laplace eigenbasis with eigenvalues \(\lambda_j\); \(\theta_0\) the truth and \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) the law of the data; Nickl’s rescaled Gaussian series prior with \(\delta_N=N^{-\gamma/(2\gamma+d)}\), and \(\tilde\delta_N(\xi)=\delta_N^{(\bar\gamma-\xi)/(\bar\gamma+1)}\) with \(\bar\gamma\) the Sobolev index of the localization ball. Let \(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\) be the linearization of \(\theta\mapsto u_\theta\) at \(\vartheta\), \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\) the heat (or Stokes) information diagonal with \(\iota_j=(1-e^{-2\nu\lambda_jT})/(2\nu\lambda_jT)\), \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_\vartheta=T^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\), \(P_J\) the projection onto the first \(J\) modes and \(\operatorname{Id}+\Xi=P_J(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\theta_0})^{-1}P_J\). Let \(\bar\theta_N\) be a preliminary estimate computed from half of the sample with \(\|\bar\theta_N-\theta_0\|_{H^\xi}=O_P(\tilde\delta_N(\xi))\) for \(0\le\xi\le\bar\gamma\), and let \(J=J_N\asymp N^{d/(2\gamma+d)}\). Let the training parameters be \[\theta^{(m)}=\bar\theta_N+N^{-1/2}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{V}_N^{1/2}\xi^{(m)},\qquad m\le M,\] where \(\mathcal{V}_N\) is a positive semidefinite \(J\times J\) matrix determined by \(Z^{(N)}\) with \(\|\mathcal{V}_N\|_{op}=O_P(1)\), and the \(\xi^{(m)}\) are i.i.d. standard normal on \(\mathbb{R}^J\) given \(Z^{(N)}\), conditioned on \(\|\xi^{(m)}\|_2^2\le4J\) when the forward map is nonlinear. Each simulated data set has its own uniform design \(X^{(m)}_i\) and noise \(\varepsilon^{(m)}_i\). With \(\phi(x)\) the \(J\times d_W\) matrix given by \(\phi(x)^\top v=(\mathbb{I}_{\bar\theta_N}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}P_J(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\bar\theta_N})^{-1}v)(x)\), rows \(\phi_j(x)^\top\), the training summary is \(\hat S^{(m)}_{N,j}=\langle e_j,\bar\theta_N\rangle+N^{-1}\sum_{i\le N}\langle\iota_j^{-1/2}\phi_j(X^{(m)}_i),Y^{(m)}_i-u_{\bar\theta_N}(X^{(m)}_i)\rangle_W\), \(j\le J\) (linearized once at \(\bar\theta_N\), population information, no prior term), and the whitened residuals are \(W^{(m)}=\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\sqrt N(P_J\theta^{(m)}-\hat S^{(m)}_N)\), used uncentered and unrescaled by the identity skip Gaussian head generator (catalog entry bernstein_von_mises_for_the_identity_skip_quantile_generator). Assumption (U2’) means: \(W^{(m)}=W^{(m)}_{g}+W^{(m)}_{r}\) with pairs i.i.d. given \(Z^{(N)}\), \(\varkappa_N=\sup_{\|v\|_2=1}\|\langle v,W^{(m)}_{g}\rangle\|_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}=O_P(1)\), where \(\|\chi\|_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}=\inf\{K>0:\mathbb{E}[e^{\chi^2/K^2}\mid Z^{(N)}]\le2\}\), and, in \(P^N_{\theta_0}\) probability, \(\mathbb{E}[\|W^{(m)}_{r}\|^2_2\mid Z^{(N)}]\to0\) and \(\|\mathbb{E}[W^{(m)}_{g}W^{(m)\top}_{g}\mid Z^{(N)}]-(\operatorname{Id}+\Xi)\|_{op}\to0\).

Conditions on the linearization, with a constant \(a_\star>0\) and, for every \(B>0\), constants \(L_\star\) and \(C_\infty\) that may depend on \(B\), required for all \(\vartheta\) with \(\|\vartheta\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}\le B\): (I1) \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\theta_0}\) is bounded on \(\ell^2\), \(K'_{\theta_0}\) is compact and \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\theta_0}\succeq a_\star\operatorname{Id}\); (I2) \(\|(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta-\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0})\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\|_{\ell^2\to L^2}\le L_\star\|\vartheta-\theta_0\|_{H^\zeta}\), where \(\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\ell^2\to L^2}=\sup_{\|g\|_2\le1}(\mathbb{E}\|(\mathcal{T}g)(X)\|^2_W)^{1/2}\) with \(X\) uniform on \(\mathcal{X}\); (I3) \(\|\mathbb{I}_\vartheta h\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal{X})}\le C_\infty\|h\|_{H^\zeta}\) for all \(h\in H^\zeta\).

Then: (a) for the heat and Stokes equations, observed from time zero, with \(\gamma>d+2\), Assumption (U2’) holds with \(\Xi=0\), \(\varkappa_N\le4/\sqrt3\) and \(W^{(m)}_{g}=-\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_m}N^{-1/2}\sum_i\phi(X^{(m)}_i)\varepsilon^{(m)}_i\), where \(\mathcal{B}_m=\{\|\hat G^{(m)}_\phi-\operatorname{Id}\|_{op}\le1\}\) and \(\hat G^{(m)}_\phi=N^{-1}\sum_{i\le N}\phi(X^{(m)}_i)\phi(X^{(m)}_i)^\top\); (b) for a nonlinear forward map, Assumption (U2’) holds under Conditions 1 and 2 of Nickl (2024) with integer \(\gamma>\max(3\zeta+3d/2+2,\eta_0)\) and \(k>\max(\zeta+5d/2,\eta_0)\) (\(\zeta>d/2\) the index of Condition 2D), \(\bar\gamma<\gamma-1\), \(\sqrt N\,\tilde\delta_N(0)\tilde\delta_N(\zeta)\to0\), and (I1) to (I3); (c) let \(\Sigma_0\) be positive definite, \(\Sigma_N=(F_{\bar\theta_N}+\Sigma_0^{-1})^{-1}\) with \(F_{\bar\theta_N}\) the realized information on \(J\) modes, and \(a_\star=1\) in case (a); under the hypotheses of (a) or (b), with probability tending to one \(N^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}F_{\bar\theta_N}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\succeq(a_\star/12)\operatorname{Id}\), and then \(\mathcal{V}_N=c^2N\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\Sigma_N\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\) has \(\|\mathcal{V}_N\|_{op}\le12c^2/a_\star\).

Source: source/chapters/ext/m3c.tex, label m:thm:u2ploc.

Linearization conditions (I1) to (I4) for the reaction diffusion class

Lemma 16.44, page 324 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Let \(\Omega=[0,1]^d\) be the torus with \(d\le3\), \(T>0\), \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\), and let \(u_\vartheta\) solve \(\partial_tu-\Delta u=f(u)\), \(u(0)=\vartheta\), with \(f\in C^\infty_c(\mathbb{R})\) (the setting of Theorem 1 of Nickl (2024), with integer \(\gamma>2+3d\)), let \(\theta_0\in C^\infty\) and \(K_f=\sup|f'|\), and let \(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta h\) be the solution \(U\) of \(\partial_tU=\Delta U+f'(u_\vartheta)U\), \(U(0)=h\). Let \(\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{diag}(1/\iota_j)\) be the heat information diagonal in the Laplace eigenbasis, \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_\vartheta=T^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathbb{I}^*_\vartheta\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\), and \(\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\ell^2\to L^2}=\sup_{\|g\|_2\le1}(\mathbb{E}|(\mathcal{T}g)(X)|^2)^{1/2}\) with \(X\) uniform on \(\mathcal{X}\). Consider the conditions, with a constant \(a_\star>0\) and, for every \(B>0\), constants \(L_\star\), \(C_\infty\), \(C_Q\) that may depend on \(B\), for all \(\vartheta,\vartheta'\) with \(\|\vartheta\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}},\|\vartheta'\|_{H^{\bar\gamma}}\le B\): (I1) \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\theta_0}\) is bounded on \(\ell^2\), \(K'_{\theta_0}\) is compact and \(\operatorname{Id}+K'_{\theta_0}\succeq a_\star\operatorname{Id}\); (I2) \(\|(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta-\mathbb{I}_{\theta_0})\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\|_{\ell^2\to L^2}\le L_\star\|\vartheta-\theta_0\|_{H^\zeta}\); (I3) \(\|\mathbb{I}_\vartheta h\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal{X})}\le C_\infty\|h\|_{H^\zeta}\) for all \(h\in H^\zeta\); (I4) \(\|u_{\vartheta'}-u_\vartheta-\mathbb{I}_\vartheta[\vartheta'-\vartheta]\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal{X})}\le C_Q\|\vartheta'-\vartheta\|^2_{H^\zeta}\). Then (I1) to (I4) hold for every \(\zeta>d/2\) and every \(\bar\gamma>\max(1,\zeta)\), and \(\|\operatorname{Id}+K'_\vartheta\|_{op}\) is bounded by a constant that depends only on \(K_f\) and \(T\).

Centered and rescaled training residuals for a nonlinear forward map with a reused design

Proposition 16.47, page 327 of Polson and Sokolov (2026), On Hilbert’s 6th Problem: Probability, Mechanics and Bayesian Computation, edition 1. New in this book.

Status: proved.

Statement. Use the setting of the theorem on the whitened residual assumption under a localized proposal (catalog entry whitened_residual_assumption_under_a_localized_proposal): data \(Y_i=u_\theta(X_i)+\varepsilon_i\) on \(\mathcal{X}=[0,T]\times\Omega\) with the observed design \((X_i)_{i\le N}\), a preliminary estimate \(\bar\theta_N\), \(J=J_N\) retained modes with projection \(P_J\), the linearization \(\mathbb{I}_\vartheta\) of \(\theta\mapsto u_\theta\) and the heat (or Stokes) information diagonal \(\mathcal{D}\). Let \(\Sigma_0\) be positive definite, \(\Sigma_N=(F_{\bar\theta_N}+\Sigma_0^{-1})^{-1}\) with \(F_{\bar\theta_N}\) the realized information on \(J\) modes, \(c>0\), and \(\theta^{(m)}=\bar\theta_N+c\Sigma_N^{1/2}\xi^{(m)}\), \(m\le M\), with the \(\xi^{(m)}\) i.i.d. standard normal on \(\mathbb{R}^J\) given the data, conditioned on \(\|\xi^{(m)}\|^2_2\le4J\). Let the simulated data sets reuse the observed design and draw fresh noise, and let \(S^{(m)}\) be their regularized one step summaries on \(J\) modes formed at \(\bar\theta_N\) with this \(\Sigma_N\), \(S^{(m)}=\bar\theta_N+\Sigma_N\big(\mathbb{I}^*_{\bar\theta_N}\big[\sum_ir^{(m)}_i\delta_{X_i}\big]-\Sigma_0^{-1}\bar\theta_N\big)\) with residuals \(r^{(m)}_i=Y^{(m)}_i-u_{\bar\theta_N}(X_i)\), and \(S(Y)\) the same summary of the observed data. Let \(Q\) be the rescaling of the localized GBC algorithm, multiplication by \((s_j+c^2p_j^2)^{-1/2}\) in the eigenbasis of \(P=\Sigma_N\Sigma_0^{-1}\), whose eigenvalues are \(p_j\), with \(s_j=1-p_j\). Then \[\Sigma_N^{-1/2}Q\big(P_J\theta^{(m)}-S^{(m)}\big)=\mu_N+W^{(m)}_{g}+W^{(m)}_{r}\] with \(\mu_N\) determined by \(Z^{(N)}\), and under the hypotheses of part (b) of that theorem (Conditions 1 and 2 of Nickl (2024) with integer \(\gamma>\max(3\zeta+3d/2+2,\eta_0)\) and \(k>\max(\zeta+5d/2,\eta_0)\), \(\bar\gamma<\gamma-1\), \(\sqrt N\,\tilde\delta_N(0)\tilde\delta_N(\zeta)\to0\), and the linearization conditions (I1) to (I3)) and the quadratic remainder condition (I4) \(\|u_{\vartheta'}-u_\vartheta-\mathbb{I}_\vartheta[\vartheta'-\vartheta]\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal{X})}\le C_Q\|\vartheta'-\vartheta\|^2_{H^\zeta}\) on \(H^{\bar\gamma}\) balls, the pairs \((W^{(m)}_{g},W^{(m)}_{r})\) satisfy Assumption (U2’) with \(\operatorname{Id}\) in place of \(\operatorname{Id}+\Xi\) (i.i.d. given the data, \(\sup_{\|v\|_2=1}\|\langle v,W^{(m)}_{g}\rangle\|_{\psi_2\mid Z^{(N)}}=O_P(1)\), \(\mathbb{E}[\|W^{(m)}_{r}\|^2_2\mid Z^{(N)}]\to0\) and \(\|\mathbb{E}[W^{(m)}_{g}W^{(m)\top}_{g}\mid Z^{(N)}]-\operatorname{Id}\|_{op}\to0\) in probability), and \(\max_m\|W^{(m)}_{r}\|_2\to0\) in probability. If \(J_N/M_N\to0\), the head of the localized GBC algorithm, centered at \(S(Y)\), produces a law \(\widehat\Pi_N\) with \(\sqrt N\,\mathscr{W}_{1,H^{-k-2}}\big(\widehat\Pi_N,N(S(Y),\Sigma_N)\big)\to0\) in probability.
